\displaystyle=\sum_{y+(c^{-1}L\cap L^{\sharp})\in c^{-1}L/(c^{-1}L\cap L^{\sharp})}\mathfrak{e}\left(cQ(y)+B(y,x)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y+(L\cap cL^{\sharp})\in L/(L\cap cL^{\sharp})}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y)+B(y,x)}{c}\right).

We now calculate
\mathfrak{g}
. Using the change of variables corresponding to the bijection

\mathscr{R}_{L/(L\cap cL^{\sharp})}\times\mathscr{R}_{(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL}\rightarrow L/cL,\quad(y_{1},y_{2})\mapsto y_{1}+y_{2}+cL,

we find that, since
B(y_{1},y_{2})/c\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\sum_{y+cL\in L/cL}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y)+B(y,x)}{c}\right)\\
=\sum_{y_{1}\in\mathscr{R}_{L/(L\cap cL^{\sharp})}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y_{1})+B(y_{1},x)}{c}\right)\cdot\sum_{y_{2}\in\mathscr{R}_{(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y_{2})+B(y_{2},x)}{c}\right).

By the condition
x+L\in D_{\underline{L}}[c]^{\bullet}
, we have
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y_{2})+B(y_{2},x)}{c}\right)=1
for all
y_{2}\in\mathscr{R}_{(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\sum_{y_{1}\in\mathscr{R}_{L/(L\cap cL^{\sharp})}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y_{1})+B(y_{1},x)}{c}\right)

\displaystyle=\lvert(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL\rvert^{-1}\sum_{y+cL\in L/cL}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{Q(y)+B(y,x)}{c}\right)

(4.4)

\displaystyle=\lvert(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL\rvert^{-1}\lvert c\rvert^{n/2}\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(1/c;x)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{Q(x)}{c}\right),

where
n=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(L)=\dim(V)
. We have
\lvert D_{\underline{L}}[c]\rvert=\lvert(c^{-1}L\cap L^{\sharp})/L\rvert=\lvert(L\cap cL^{\sharp})/cL\rvert
, and
\lvert D_{\underline{\Lambda}}[c]\rvert=\lvert(c^{-1}\Lambda\cap\Lambda^{\sharp})/\Lambda\rvert=\lvert c\rvert^{n}
since
\Lambda\subseteq cL
. Inserting these two equations and (
4.4
) into (
4.3
) yields

\mathscr{G}_{\underline{\Lambda}}(c,x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert D_{\underline{L}}[c]\rvert}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{Q(x)}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(1/c;x)\mathscr{G}_{\underline{L}}(c,x),\quad\text{if }\Lambda\subseteq cL.

To deduce (
4.1
) from the above formula, it remains to prove

(4.5)

\mathscr{G}_{\underline{\Lambda}}(c,x)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(c\right)}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{Q(x)}{c}\right)

for some
9
9

9

Indeed, if we can prove (
4.5
) for one lattice
\Lambda\subseteq cL
, then it will hold for all lattices
\Lambda\subseteq cL
.
lattice
\Lambda\subseteq cL
. To this end, we choose
n
mutually orthogonal nonzero vectors
e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{n}\in cL
. Then they form a basis of
V
. Set
\Lambda=\bigoplus_{j}\mathbb{Z}e_{j}
; it is a lattice in
(V,B)
and
\Lambda\subseteq cL
. We now calculate (
4.5
) for this particular
\Lambda
. Set
d_{j}=B(e_{j},e_{j})
. Since
\Lambda\subseteq cL
and
L
is even integral, we have
2c^{2}\mid d_{j}
. Let
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
be the numbers of
j
such that
d_{j}/c>0
and
d_{j}/c<0
, respectively. Then
n_{1}-n_{2}=\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(c\right)
. Set
x=\sum_{j}r_{j}e_{j}
, where
r_{j}\in\mathbb{Q}
. We have
\lvert\Lambda^{\sharp}/\Lambda\rvert=\prod_{j}\lvert d_{j}\rvert
and
\lvert\Lambda^{\sharp}/\Lambda[c]\rvert=\lvert c\rvert^{n}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\prod\nolimits_{j}\lvert d_{j}\rvert}\cdot\sqrt{\lvert c\rvert^{n}}\cdot\mathscr{G}_{\underline{\Lambda}}(c,x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\Lambda^{\sharp}/\Lambda}\mathfrak{e}\left(cQ(y)+B(y,x)\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{y_{j}\in d_{j}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{e}\left(cQ(y_{j}e_{j})+B(y_{j}e_{j},x)\right)

(4.6)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/d_{j}\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{c}{2d_{j}}y^{2}+r_{j}y\right).

Since
e_{j}\in cL
and
x\in L^{\sharp}
, we have
c^{-1}r_{j}d_{j}=B(c^{-1}e_{j},x)\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/d_{j}\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{c}{2d_{j}}y^{2}+r_{j}y\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/d_{j}\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{c}{2d_{j}}\left(y+\frac{d_{j}r_{j}}{c}\right)^{2}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{d_{j}r_{j}^{2}}{2c}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert c\rvert}{2}\sum_{y\bmod{\lvert 2d_{j}/c\rvert}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{y^{2}}{2d_{j}/c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{d_{j}r_{j}^{2}}{2c}\right).

Inserting the classical formula of Gauss (see (
1.7
)) into the above formula, we have, since
4\mid 2d_{j}/c
,

\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}/d_{j}\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{c}{2d_{j}}y^{2}+r_{j}y\right)=\sqrt{\lvert d_{j}\rvert}\sqrt{\lvert c\rvert}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{d_{j}r_{j}^{2}}{2c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{j}/c\right)}{8}\right).

Substituting this into (
4.6
) gives (
4.5
), as desired.

For the last statement,
cQ(z)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
z+L\in D_{\underline{L}}[c]
implies that
0+L\in D_{\underline{L}}[c]^{\bullet}
, and hence (
4.1
) holds for
x=0
.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

Let
N
be the level of
\underline{L}
. If
\gcd(c,N)=1
, then
D_{\underline{L}}[c]=\{0+L\}
and
D_{\underline{L}}[c]^{\bullet}=D_{\underline{L}}
. Therefore, (
4.1
) holds for all
x\in L^{\sharp}
. On the other hand, if
N\mid c
, then
cQ(z)=c/N\cdot NQ(z)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
z+L\in D_{\underline{L}}
, and hence (
4.1
) holds for
x=0
.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
\underline{L}=(L,B)
be an even integral lattice of signature
\sigma
and level
N
. Let
c
be a nonzero integer divisible by
N
. Then

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(1/c;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(c\right)}{8}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert}.

Proof.

By the above remark, (
4.1
) is applicable for
x=0
, which gives

(4.7)

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(1/c;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(c\right)}{8}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\lvert D_{\underline{L}}[c]\rvert}\mathscr{G}_{\underline{L}}(c,0)^{-1}.

Since
N\mid c
, we have
D_{\underline{L}}[c]=D_{\underline{L}}
, and
\mathscr{G}_{\underline{L}}(c,0)=1
. Inserting these into (
4.7
) gives the desired conclusion.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
\underline{L}=(L,B)
be an even integral lattice of signature
\sigma
and level
N
. Set
D=\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert
. Let
b
,
d
be integers with
d\neq 0
,
\gcd(b,d)=1
and
\gcd(N,d)=1
. Then

(4.8)

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{0}\right)-d_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}^n D}{d}_{K},

where
d_{0}
is the odd part of
d
(with
\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{0}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d\right)
),
n=\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}(L)
, and where we set
\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(0\right)=0
as a convention.

Proof.

If
b=0
, then
d=\pm 1
, and the conclusion holds trivially. Now assume that
b\neq 0
. According to Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{G}_{(L,\lvert b\rvert\cdot B)}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)/d;0)

(4.9)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(bd\right)}{8}\right)\sqrt{\lvert D_{(L,\lvert b\rvert\cdot B)}[\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)d]\rvert}\mathscr{G}_{(L,\lvert b\rvert\cdot B)}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)d,0)^{-1},

where we have used the fact that the signatures of
\underline{L}
and of
(L,\lvert b\rvert B)
, respectively, are the same. Note that the level of
(L,\lvert b\rvert B)
is equal to
\lvert b\rvert N
. By the conditions we have
\gcd(\lvert b\rvert N,\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)d)=1
, and hence
\sqrt{\lvert D_{(L,\lvert b\rvert\cdot B)}[\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)d]\rvert}=1
. Inserting this and (
3.17
) into (
4.9
) gives

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(bd\right)}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{(b^{-1}L^\sharp)/L}}{\sgn{b}d}_{K}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{\sigma\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)d_{0}}{8}\right).

The above formula can be simplified to (
4.8
) since
\lvert(b^{-1}L^{\sharp})/L\rvert=\lvert b\rvert^{n}D
and since
\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{x}{-y}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{x}{y}_{K}
when
x>0
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let the notations and assumptions be as in Lemma
3.7
. Suppose that
\gcd(N,a)=1
. If
N\mid b
and
d\neq 0
, then we have

(4.10)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d\right)}{2}}c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)\\
\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)\sqrt{\lvert(G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap c^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rvert}\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})),

where
a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
is any integer with
aa^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{cN}
.

Proof.

Since
N\mid b
, we have
bc\cdot L^{\sharp}\subseteq L
, and hence Proposition
3.17
is applicable. Substituting (
3.6
) (with
y=\mathbf{w}
and
x=-aG^{-1}\mathbf{x}
) into (
3.3
) gives

(4.11)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{2}d}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)(\mathrm{i}c)^{\frac{n}{2}}\sqrt{\det(G)}\\
\cdot(-\mathrm{i})^{\frac{n(1-\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d\right))}{2}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;\mathbf{w})\sqrt{\frac{\lvert D_{\underline{L}}[c]\rvert}{\lvert D_{\underline{L}}\rvert}}\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-cd,\mathbf{w}+adG^{-1}\mathbf{x}).

We have
D_{\underline{L}}=G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, and hence

\lvert D_{\underline{L}}\rvert=\det(G),\quad\lvert D_{\underline{L}}[c]\rvert=\lvert(G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap c^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rvert.

Since
bc\cdot L^{\sharp}\subseteq L
and
\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}=L^{\sharp}
, we have
bc\mathbf{w}\in L
, and hence Proposition
3.16
is valid, that is, (
3.5
) holds. However
N\mid b
, so we can simplify (
3.5
) to

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;w)=\mathfrak{e}\left(a^{2}bdQ(w)\right)\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0).

Furthermore, we have

\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{2}d}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}ad}{2cN}(a\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}NG^{-1}(a\mathbf{x})\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)

for
ad=1+bc
and
\frac{ba^{2}}{2}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Now we consider
\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-cd,\mathbf{w}+adG^{-1}\mathbf{x})
. Applying the change of variables
D_{\underline{L}}\rightarrow D_{\underline{L}}
,
y\mapsto ay
, which is bijective since
N\mid b
, we find that

\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-cd,\mathbf{w}+adG^{-1}\mathbf{x})=\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-a^{2}cd,a\mathbf{w}+a^{2}dG^{-1}\mathbf{x})=\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})),

where we have used the fact
D_{\underline{L}}[-cd]=D_{\underline{L}}[-a^{2}cd]=D_{\underline{L}}[-ac]
. Taking into account these facts, we can reduce (
4.11
) to (
4.10
), as desired.
∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

When
a=0
, then
c=1
and Theorem
1.1
holds trivially. Assume that
a\neq 0
below. First we consider the subcase
\gcd(N,a)=1
.

We begin with (
4.10
), which is true for under the conditions of Theorem
1.1
,
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})
is indeed integral-parametric, and we can indeed require
b
and
d
fulfilling the further requirement
N\mid b
and
d\neq 0
, for
\gcd(N,a)=1
. Since
N\mid b
, we have
\gcd(N,d)=1
, and hence (
4.8
) holds with
\sigma=n
. It remains to calculate
\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x}))
in (
4.10
).

If
\gcd(N,c)=1
, then (
4.1
), with

replaced by
-ac
, holds for all

by Remark
4.2
, and hence for

. It follows that

(4.12)

\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x}))=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)}{8}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}\left(-\frac{1}{ac};a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)^{-1}.

Let
a_{1},b_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfy
\det\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{a_{1}}&{1}\\
{Nb_{1}}&{-ac}\end{smallmatrix}\right)=1
. Then
Nb_{1}a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and hence, by Proposition
3.16
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}\left(-\frac{1}{ac};a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a_{1}^{2}a^{3}c}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}\left(-\frac{1}{ac};0\right)

(4.13)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{2}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}\left(-\frac{1}{ac};0\right),

where

satisfy
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN}
. Recall that
aa^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{cN}
as required in (
4.10
). Again by Corollary
4.4
we have

(4.14)

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}\left(-\frac{1}{ac};0\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(-a_{0}c_{0}\right)+a_{0}c_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{-ac}_{K},

where
a_{0}
and
c_{0}
are the odd part of
a
and
c
, respectively. Inserting (
4.14
) into (
4.13
), and then inserting the obtained formula into (
4.12
), we find that

\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x}))\\
=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{2}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(-a_{0}c_{0}\right)+a_{0}c_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{-ac}_{K}.

Inserting this and (
4.8
) into (
4.10
), and noting that
\lvert(G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap c^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rvert=1
since
\gcd(N,c)=1
, we deduce that, in the case where
\gcd(N,a)=\gcd(N,c)=1
,

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d\right)}{2}}c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{0}\right)-d_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}^n D}{d}_{K}\\
\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{2}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{n(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(-a_{0}c_{0}\right)+a_{0}c_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{-ac}_{K}.

When choosing
b
and
d
in (
4.10
), we can further require that
d>0
,
2\nmid d
and
d
is sufficiently large, so that
\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(bc\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)
and
d_{0}=d
. Then the above formula can be reduced to

(4.15)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}+\frac{a^{\prime}c^{\prime}a^{2}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\\
\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n(1-a_{0}c_{0})}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{-ac}_{K}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-d)}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}^n D}{d}_{K}.

Setting
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{x}=0
in the above we deduce that

(4.16)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;0,0)=c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n(1-a_{0}c_{0})}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{-ac}_{K}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-d)}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}^n D}{d}_{K}.

On the other hand, we have

(4.17)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;0,0)=c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(a/c;0)=c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}^n D}{c}_{K}

by Corollary
4.4
. Thus the right-hand sides of (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) are equal. Inserting this equality into (
4.15
), we arrive at (
1.4
), as desired.

This concludes the proof in the case
\gcd(N,c)=\gcd(N,a)=1
. Now assume
\gcd(N,c)=1
but
a
is general. According to Proposition
3.6
, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\mathfrak{G}_{\lvert a\rvert G}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)/c;\mathbf{w},\lvert a\rvert\mathbf{x})
. Since
\gcd(N,\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right))=1
, we can apply what we have proved to
\mathfrak{G}_{\lvert a\rvert G}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)/c;\mathbf{w},\lvert a\rvert\mathbf{x})
. It should be noted that the level of
\lvert a\rvert G
is
\lvert a\rvert N
and
\det(\lvert a\rvert G)=\lvert a\rvert^{n}D
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{G}_{\lvert a\rvert G}(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)/c;\mathbf{w},\lvert a\rvert\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)}{2c}(\lvert a\rvert\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}(\lvert a\rvert G)^{-1}(\lvert a\rvert\mathbf{x})+\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)c^{\prime\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}\lvert a\rvert G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}^n D}{c}_{K},

where
c^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfies
cc^{\prime\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot\lvert a\rvert N}
. This formula can be reduced to (
1.4
) immediately.

Finally, we shall treat the case
\gcd(N,c)=N
, in which we must have
\gcd(N,a)=1
so that (
4.10
) still holds. Since
N\mid c
, we have
\lvert(G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap c^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rvert=\lvert G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rvert=D
. Therefore,

(4.18)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d\right)}{2}}c^{\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)\sqrt{D}\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})).

It follows from the orthogonality relation that
\mathscr{G}_{D_{\underline{L}}}(-ac,a(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x}))=\delta
where
\delta
is the one defined in Theorem
1.1
(2). It remains to express
\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)
in terms of
a
and
c
in (
4.18
). Recall that when choosing
b
and
d
, we require
ad-bc=1
,
N\mid b
, and
d\neq 0
. We now split the proof into four cases:

(i)\,2\mid D\qquad(ii)\,2\nmid D\text{ and }2\mid c\qquad(iii)\,2\nmid D,2\nmid c\text{ and }2\nmid a\qquad(iv)\,2\nmid D,2\nmid c\text{ and }2\mid a.

For case (i), we further require that
b
and
d
satisfy
4D\mid b
(which is possible for
N
and
4D
have the same set of prime factors),
d>0
and
\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)
. Then we have
\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}}{d}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}}{a}_{K}
for
ad\equiv 1\bmod{\lvert b\rvert}
and
4\mid b
, and we have
\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{d}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{a}_{K}
for
ad\equiv 1\bmod{4D}
. It follows from these facts and (
4.8
) that

(4.19)

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-a)}{8}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}}{a}_{K}^{n}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{a}_{K}

It can be proved that
\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}}{a}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{-\sgn{a}c}{\abs{a}}_{K}
using the facts
-bc\equiv 1\bmod{a}
,
\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)
and
2\nmid a
. Inserting this into (
4.19
) we find that
\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mu_{G}(a,c)
, where
\mu_{G}(a,c)
is the one defined in Theorem
1.1
(2). Combining this and (
4.18
) we arrive at (
1.5
), as desired.

For case (ii), we must have
2\mid n
(cf., e.g.
[
17
, Remarks 14.3.23(b)]
). The deduction used in case (i) is also valid for this case.

For case (iii), we must have
2\mid n
as in case (ii). The deduction is similar to case (ii). The different point is that in this case, we shall use the fact
2\mid n
when proving

\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{0}\right)-d_{0})}{8}\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)(1-a)}{8}\right).

(If one chooses
b
with
8D\mid b
instead of
4D\mid b
, which is possible, then one does not need the fact
2\mid n
to prove the above equality.)

For case (iv), we also have
2\mid n
as above. We further require that
b
and
d
satisfy

D\mid b,\quad d>0,\quad d\equiv 1\bmod{8},\quad\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right).

Such
b
and
d
always exist by modulo
8
arithmetic and by the fact
D
and
N
have the same set of prime factors. Since
2\mid a
and
ad-bc=1
, we have
b\equiv c\equiv 1\bmod{2}
. Therefore, (
4.8
) implies

\mathfrak{G}_{\underline{L}}(b/d;0)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(b\right)(\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(d_{0}\right)-d_{0})}{8}\right)\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{b}^n D}{d}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{d}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{a}{D}_{K}.

Combining this and (
4.18
) we arrive at (
1.5
), as desired. This concludes the whole proof.

4.3.
When
G
is indefinite

If we replace the assumption “
G
is positive definite” by “
G
is nonsingular” in Theorem
1.1
, then there are similar conclusions. For instance, Corollaries
4.3
and
4.4
give formulas for
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(r;0,0)
. These formulas involve the signature of the corresponding lattice and involve
\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert=\lvert\det(G)\rvert
. Attention must be paid to that in Theorem
1.1
we define
D=\det(G)
, but in the above propositions where
\underline{L}
or
G
are allowed to be non-positive-definite,
D
usually means
\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert=\lvert\det(G)\rvert
.

The following extension of the
\gcd(N,c)=1
case of Theorem
1.1
to indefinite
G
is interesting, for the formula does not involve the signature. This formula is more efficient than those involving the signature, e.g., Corollary
4.4
, as to calculate the signature (indeed, only signature mod
8
is needed), one need to first calculate all eigenvalues of
G
or calculate the Jordan decomposition of
G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, both of which, when
n
is large, are more arduous tasks than just calculating
\det(G)
and then inserting it into the right-hand side of (
1.4
).

Theorem 4.6
.

Let the notations and assumptions be as in Theorem
1.1
except that
G
is not required to be positive definite but required merely to be nonsingular. If
\gcd(N,c)=1
, then (
1.4
) still holds. (We emphasize that
D=\det(G)
may be negative now, and that
\gcd(N,c)=1\Longleftrightarrow\gcd(D,c)=1
.)

Proof.

Let
t
be a positive integer and set
G_{p}=G+4N^{2}ct\cdot I_{n}
, where
I_{n}
is the identity matrix. Then
G_{p}
is even integral and symmetric. Let
t
be sufficiently large, then
G_{p}
is positive definite. Let
N_{p}
be the level of
G_{p}
and let
D_{p}=\det(G_{p})
. Then

(4.20)

D_{p}\equiv D\bmod{4N^{2}c},

and hence

\gcd(N,c)=1\Longleftrightarrow\gcd(D,c)=1\Longleftrightarrow\gcd(D_{p},c)=1\Longleftrightarrow\gcd(N_{p},c)=1.

Since
G\cdot\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we have
G_{p}\cdot\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, so that
\mathfrak{G}_{G_{p}}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})
is integral-parametric. By the definition, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{G_{p}}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{c}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G\cdot(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})+(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot\mathbf{x}\right)+2at(N\mathbf{v}+N\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}(N\mathbf{v}+N\mathbf{w})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})

since
2at(N\mathbf{v}+N\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}(N\mathbf{v}+N\mathbf{w})\in\mathbb{Z}
. Taking into account this and applying Theorem
1.1
(1) to
\mathfrak{G}_{G_{p}}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})
, we obtain

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=c^{n/2}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{c}_{K}\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\\
\times\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x}+\frac{ac^{\prime\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}(\mathbf{w}+G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right),

where
c^{\prime\prime}
is any integer satisfying
cc^{\prime\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN_{p}}
. To deduce (
1.4
) from the above formula, it remains to prove

(4.21)

\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{c}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K},

and

(4.22)

\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x}+\frac{ac^{\prime\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}(\mathbf{w}+G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right)\\
=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}+\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}+G^{-1}\mathbf{x})\right),

where
c^{\prime}
is any integer satisfying
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN}
.

If
c
is odd (it is already positive), then the function
x\mapsto\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{x}{c}_{K}
is
c
-periodic, and hence (
4.21
) holds due to (
4.20
). Otherwise, if
c
is even, then we can write
c=2^{e}c_{0}
where
e\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
c_{0}
is odd. By (
4.20
) we have
D_{p}\equiv D\bmod{8}
, and hence
\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{2}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{2}_{K}
. It follows that

\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{c}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{2}_{K}^{e}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D_p}{c_0}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{2}_{K}^{e}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c_0}_{K}=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}.

This completes the proof of (
4.21
).

Now we treat (
4.22
). We choose a
c^{\prime\prime\prime}
satisfying
cc^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aNN_{p}}
, which exists since
\gcd(aNN_{p},c)=1
. Then the left-hand and right-hand sides of (
4.22
) remain unchanged if we set
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
c^{\prime\prime}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}
, respectively. After setting this value, we find (
4.22
) is equivalent to

(4.23)

\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{ac^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}\mathbf{w}+ac^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{a(cc^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right)\\
=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{ac^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}+ac^{\prime\prime\prime}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{x}+\frac{a(cc^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right).

Since
G_{p}-G=4N^{2}ctI_{n}
, we have

(4.24)

\frac{ac^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}\mathbf{w}-\frac{ac^{\prime\prime\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}=2acc^{\prime\prime\prime}t(N\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}(N\mathbf{w})\in\mathbb{Z}.

Note that
G_{p}^{-1}-G^{-1}=-4N^{2}ctG_{p}^{-1}G^{-1}
. Therefore, if we let
cc^{\prime\prime\prime}=1+aNN_{p}u
with
u\in\mathbb{Z}
, then

(4.25)

\frac{a(cc^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G_{p}^{-1}\mathbf{x}-\frac{a(cc^{\prime\prime\prime}-1)}{2c}\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{x}=-2N^{2}ut(a\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}(N_{p}G_{p}^{-1})(NG^{-1})(a\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{Z}.

Combining (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), we deduce (
4.23
), and hence (
4.22
). This concludes the proof.
∎

5.
Application I. A duality theorem and Gauss subsums

In this section, we give our first application of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
4.6
, namely, some duality formulas on Gauss subsums. The results are presented in two theorems, according to
\gcd(N,c)=1
and
N
respectively.

The conclusion for the case
\gcd(N,c)=1
has been presented in Theorem
1.3
, whose proof will be given below in §
5.2
. The conclusion for the case
\gcd(N,c)=N
, together with its proof, will be given in §
5.3
. A lemma which is valid for all
c
is provided in §
5.1
. It is required for both cases just mentioned.

5.1.
A lemma for general
c

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
G
be a nonsingular even integral symmetric matrix of size
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
. Let
c
be a positive integer and
a
be a nonzero integer coprime to
c
. Let
H
be a subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and let
\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then we have

(5.1)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{c^{n}}\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}}\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in H^{\bot}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\mathbf{y}).

Before the proof, we remark three important facts. Firstly,
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})
showing up in the left-hand side of (
5.1
) is well-defined by Corollary
3.3
, since here
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w})
is integral-parametric. Secondly, each
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\mathbf{y})
showing up in the right-hand side of (
5.1
) actually means
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}})
where
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
is any element in the preimage of
\mathbf{y}
under the projection
\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. One can show immediately that this quantity is independent of the choice of
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
. However, the values of both factors
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}}{c}\right)
and
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{y}})
themselves may change if we choose different
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}
. Finally, the sum
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\mathbf{y})
is of-course integral-parametric.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Let
f
be the function on
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
defined by
f(\mathbf{v})=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})\right)
. For each
\mathbf{t}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, let
\chi_{\mathbf{t}}
be the character of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
defined by
\chi_{\mathbf{t}}(\mathbf{v})=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{1}{c}\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{t}\right)
. It is immediate that the map from
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
to
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}
that sends
\mathbf{t}
to
\chi_{\mathbf{t}}
is a group isomorphism. Applying Theorem
2.8
to
M=\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
,
x=0
and to
f
just defined, we obtain

(5.2)

\frac{1}{\lvert H\rvert}\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})=\frac{1}{\lvert H\rvert}\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in H}f(\mathbf{y})=\frac{1}{c^{n}}\sum_{\chi\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}^{H}}\widehat{f}(\chi)\overline{\chi(0)}.

By the definitions and Lemma
3.2
, for each
\mathbf{x}\in a^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
we have

(5.3)

\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}f(\mathbf{v})\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}(a\mathbf{x})}{c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{x}}{c}\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{x}}{c}\right)\widehat{f}(\chi_{-a\mathbf{x}}).

Let
\mathscr{R}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}
be a complete set of representatives of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then when
\mathbf{x}
runs over the elements in
-a^{-1}\cdot\mathscr{R}
,
\chi_{-a\mathbf{x}}
precisely runs over all characters in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}
. Combining this, (
5.2
) and (
5.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert H\rvert}\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c^{n}}\cdot\sum_{-a\mathbf{x}\in\mathscr{R},\,\chi_{-a\mathbf{x}}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}^{H}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{x}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})

(5.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c^{n}}\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathscr{R},\,\chi_{\mathbf{y}}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}^{H}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},-a^{-1}\mathbf{y}).

According to (
2.3
), we have

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathbf{y}}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}^{H}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\text{for all }(v_{1},v_{2},\dots,v_{n})+\mathbb{Z}^{n}\in H\text{ we have }\sum v_{i}y_{i}\equiv 0\bmod{c}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\mathbf{y}+\mathbb{Z}^{n}\in H^{\bot}.

Inserting this into (
5.4
), and noting that
c^{n}=\sqrt{c^{n}}\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}
(see Corollary
2.5
), we arrive at (
5.1
), as desired.
∎

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Note that both
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w})
and
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w})
are integral-parametric, so
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})
and
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w})
are well-defined by Corollary
3.3
. In other words, each factor in (
1.10
) makes sense. Since
\gcd(N,c)=1
, we can apply Theorem
4.6
. Therefore, we substitute (
1.4
) into the right-hand side of (
5.1
) and obtain

(5.5)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}}\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in H^{\bot}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\\
\times\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{1}{2ac}\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}-a^{-1}G^{-1}\mathbf{y})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}-a^{-1}G^{-1}\mathbf{y})\right),

where
c_{0}
is the odd part of
c
, and
c^{\prime}
is any solution of
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN}
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{1}{2ac}\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}-a^{-1}G^{-1}\mathbf{y})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}-a^{-1}G^{-1}\mathbf{y})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{cc^{\prime}-1}{2ac}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}}{2c}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})\right).

Inserting this into the right-hand side of (
5.5
), we arrive at (
1.10
), which finishes the proof.

5.3.
The
\gcd(N,c)=N
case

Let the notations and assumptions be the same as in Theorem
1.3
, but now we assume that
G
is positive definite, and
N\mid c
. The coset
aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}
is contained in
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Set

(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}:=\{\mathbf{x}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\colon\mathbf{x}\in aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}\},

which is a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then the
N\mid c
analog of Theorem
1.3
is the following one.

Theorem 5.2
.

In the case
G
is positive definite and
N\mid c
, we have
10
10

10

It may happen that
H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}=\emptyset
, in which case
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc},\mathbf{c};-aG\mathbf{w},0\right)=0
.

(5.6)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}\left(\frac{a}{c};\mathbf{w}\right)=\gamma\cdot\frac{\sqrt{D}}{\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc},\mathbf{c};-aG\mathbf{w},0\right),

where
a^{\prime}
is any integer with
aa^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{cN}
,
\mathbf{c}:=(c,c,\dots,c)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, and where

\gamma=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n}{2}}\cdot\mu_{G}(a,c)\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)

with
\mu_{G}(a,c)
given by (
1.6
).

Before the proof, it is important to notice that
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc},\mathbf{c};-aG\mathbf{w},0\right)
is not necessarily integral-parametric, since in general
\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc}\cdot\mathbf{c}\not\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. However, an analog of Lemma
3.2
holds, which ensures
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc},\mathbf{c};-aG\mathbf{w},0\right)
to be well-defined.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
p\colon\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
be the natural quotient map. Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{n}
be a subset such that
p|_{S_{j}}
is a bijection from
S
onto
H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then the value of

(5.7)

\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in S}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{v}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\right)

is independent of the choice of
S
. In other words,
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc},\mathbf{c};-aG\mathbf{w},0\right)
is independent of the choice of representatives of the elements of
H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, and hence is well-defined.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}
be vectors such that
p(\mathbf{v}_{1})=p(\mathbf{v}_{2})\in H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then

\mathbf{v}_{1},\,\mathbf{v}_{2}\in aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}=aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}\,(\text{since }c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\subseteq G\mathbb{Z}^{n}),

and
\Delta\mathbf{v}:=\mathbf{v}_{2}-\mathbf{v}_{1}\in c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}_{2}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{v}_{2}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{c}\Delta\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2c}\Delta\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\Delta\mathbf{v}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{-a^{\prime}}{Nc}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\right),

for
\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2c}\Delta\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\Delta\mathbf{v}\right)=1
since
-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2c}\Delta\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\Delta\mathbf{v}=-a^{\prime}\cdot\frac{1}{2}(c^{-1}\Delta\mathbf{v})^{\mathrm{T}}cG^{-1}(c^{-1}\Delta\mathbf{v})\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{c}\Delta\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)=1
since
\frac{\Delta\mathbf{v}}{c}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
G^{-1}(\mathbf{v}_{1}-aG\mathbf{w})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. That is to say, each term in (
5.7
) is independent of the choice of the representative
\mathbf{v}
, so the whole sum (
5.7
) is independent of the choice of
S
.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
5.2
.

Proof of Theorem
5.2
.

Inserting (
1.5
) into the right-hand side of (
5.1
), we obtain

(5.8)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n}{2}}B\frac{\sqrt{D}}{\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}}\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{y}\in H^{\bot}}\delta_{a\mathbf{w}-G^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\mathbb{Z}^{n},0+\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2c}\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{y}\right),

where
\delta_{a\mathbf{w}-G^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\mathbb{Z}^{n},0+\mathbb{Z}^{n}}
is the Kronecker delta. We have

\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{y}}{c}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2c}\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{-1}\mathbf{y}\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2Nc}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right).

Inserting this into the right-hand side of (
5.8
), and noting
a\mathbf{w}-G^{-1}\mathbf{y}+\mathbb{Z}^{n}=0+\mathbb{Z}^{n}
if and only if
\mathbf{y}\in aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we deduce that

(5.9)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lvert H\rvert}}\cdot\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})=\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n}{2}}B\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)\frac{\sqrt{D}}{\sqrt{\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert}}\\
\cdot\sum_{\mathbf{y}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\in H^{\bot},\,\mathbf{y}\in aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{a^{\prime}}{2Nc}(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G^{\bot}\cdot(\mathbf{y}-aG\mathbf{w})\right).

It is immediate that
\mathrm{i}^{\frac{n}{2}}B\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\prime}a^{2}}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)=\gamma
, since
aa^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{cN}
. Thus, (
5.9
) can be reduced to the desired conclusion.
∎

6.
Intermission: Examples of the main theorem and the duality theorem

In this section, we give some concrete examples, immediate corollaries, and direct applications of Theorems
1.1
,
4.6
,
1.3
and
5.2
.

6.1.
The
A_{n}
lattices

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and let
G=A_{n}
be the matrix whose
(i,j)
-entry equals
2
if
i=j
, equals
-1
if
\lvert i-j\rvert=1
, and equals
0
otherwise. It is positive definite by, e.g., completing the squares. The lattices
(\mathbb{Z}^{n},(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mapsto\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}A_{n}\mathbf{y})
and
(\mathbb{Z}^{n},(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mapsto\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}A_{n}^{-1}\mathbf{y})
are similar to the weight lattice and the root lattice of the root system of type
A_{n}
, respectively; cf., e.g.
[
47
, p. 254 and 264]
.

Note that the entries of
A_{n}^{-1}
can be determined explicitly as follows. If
i\leq j
, then the
(i,j)
-entry of
A_{n}^{-1}
equals
\frac{i(n+1-j)}{n+1}
. Other entries are determined by the fact
A_{n}^{-1}
is symmetric (since
A_{n}
is). It follows that the level of
A_{n}
is equal to
2n+2
if
n
is odd, and equal to
n+1
if
n
is even. Moreover, since
\det(A_{n})=2\det(A_{n-1})-\det(A_{n-2})
for
n\geq 3
and
\det(A_{1})=2
,
\det(A_{2})=3
, we have
\det(A_{n})=n+1
by induction. For instance, the matrices
A_{5}
and
A_{5}^{-1}
are provided below:

A_{5}=\begin{pmatrix}2&-1&0&0&0\\
-1&2&-1&0&0\\
0&-1&2&-1&0\\
0&0&-1&2&-1\\
0&0&0&-1&2\end{pmatrix},\quad 6A_{5}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}5&4&3&2&1\\
4&8&6&4&2\\
3&6&9&6&3\\
2&4&6&8&4\\
1&2&3&4&5\end{pmatrix}.

For
G=A_{n}
, the formula (
1.4
) with
\mathbf{w}=0
becomes

\sum_{v_{1},\dots,v_{n}\bmod{c}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{c}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}v_{i}v_{i+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}x_{i}\right)\right)=c^{n/2}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{n+1}{c}_{K}\\
\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a(cc^{\prime}-1)}{2c(n+1)}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}i(n+1-i)x_{i}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}i(n+1-j)x_{i}x_{j}\right)\right),

provided that
c>0
,
\gcd(c,n+1)=\gcd(c,a)=1
and
ax_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i
.

6.2.
A condition for the vanishing of Gauss subsums

Here is an interesting application of Theorem
5.2
, which gives a criterion for whether
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{S}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
, where
S
is a certain small subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. This gives an insight into the fact that when
\mathfrak{G}_{G}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
, this sum can be divided into many subsums, all of which vanish.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
G
be a positive definite even integral symmetric matrix of size
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and let
N
be its level. Let
a
be a nonzero integer and
c
be a positive integer with
\gcd(a,c)=1
and
N\mid c
. Let
\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
satisfying
a\mathbf{w}\not\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
.

(1)

If
H
is a subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
containing
cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, then
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
.

(2)

For all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we have
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{(\mathbf{x}+cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
.

Proof.

(1) Note that

\displaystyle(G\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{\bot}

\displaystyle=\{\mathbf{x}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\colon\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{x}\in c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\text{ for all }\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\}=cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n},

and that

H\supseteq(G\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n})^{\bot}\Longleftrightarrow H^{\bot}\subseteq G\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}

by the second conclusion of Lemma
2.3
. Since
a\mathbf{w}\not\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we have

H^{\bot}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\subseteq G\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\cap(aG\mathbf{w}+G\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}=\emptyset.

Thus, the right-hand side of (
5.6
) vanishes. This, together with Theorem
5.2
, implies that
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
, as desired.

(2) Let
H=cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then
(\mathbf{x}+cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}=(\mathbf{x}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n})+H
, which is an element of
(\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n})/H
. Thus, we have

\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{(\mathbf{x}+cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{(\mathbf{x}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n})+H}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}\left(a/c;\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{w}\right),

where the latter identity follows from the definition. Since
\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
a\mathbf{w}\not\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, we have as well
\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
a(\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{w})\not\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Therefore, the first conclusion we have just proved implies
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}\left(a/c;\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{w}\right)=0
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{(\mathbf{x}+cG^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n})/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\left(a/c;\mathbf{w}\right)=0
.
∎

These are not strong conclusions, since one can also prove them in a rather direct manner, using the orthogonality relation of characters of
G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}/\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. However, they tell us that in the case
N\mid c
and
\delta=0
in (
1.5
), what the pattern of cancellations in
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x})
looks like: this sum actually can be divided into
c^{n}/\det(G)
sums, each of which equals
0
due to the orthogonality relation mentioned above.

6.3.
Explicit formulas of Gauss subsums

If
H^{\bot}
in (
1.10
) is cyclic, then the right-hand side is a classical Gauss sum, of which explicit formulas are known. Thus, we will have explicit formulas for
\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})
for such
H
. Note that for
\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
or
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, by
\mathbf{v}_{1}\perp\mathbf{v}_{2}
we understand
\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{v}_{2}\equiv 0\bmod{c}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
G
be a nonsingular even integral symmetric matrix of size
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and let
D
and
N
be the determinant and level of
G
, respectively. Let
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{\neq 0}
with
\gcd(c,a)=\gcd(c,N)=1
. Let
\mathbf{w}\in G^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
\mathbf{h}=(h_{1},\dots,h_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Set
u=c/\gcd(c,h_{1},\dots,h_{n})
. Then we have

(6.1)

\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n},\,\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{h}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{c}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G\cdot(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})\right)\right)\\
=c^{n/2}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)\cdot C,

where
c_{0}
is the odd part of
c
, and
c^{\prime}
is any integer satisfying
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN}
, and where

C=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\equiv 0\bmod{2c}\text{ and }\mathbf{h}\perp N\mathbf{w}\\
0&\text{if }\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\equiv 0\bmod{2c}\text{ and }\mathbf{h}\not\perp N\mathbf{w}\\
u^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{(2)}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c},u;0,-\frac{2aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}\right)&\text{if }\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\not\equiv 0\bmod{2c},\end{cases}

with
a^{\bot}
an integer subject to the condition
a^{\bot}aN\equiv-1\bmod{c}
.

Some remarks are in order before the proof. First, the integer
c^{\prime}
can be chosen such that merely
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{N}
. The requirement
cc^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{aN}
is presented here for being consistent with its usage in (
1.4
). In addition, it is immediate that
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h},\,N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{Z}
, that
2c\mid u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}
, and that
c\mid uN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}
. Therefore

(6.2)

\mathfrak{G}_{(2)}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c},u;0,-\frac{2aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{(2)}\left(\frac{a_{1}}{c_{1}},u;0,-\frac{2aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}\right),

where
a_{1}=\frac{a^{\bot}u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}/\gcd\left(u,\frac{u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}\right)
and
c_{1}=u/\gcd\left(u,\frac{u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}\right)
are coprime integers. Since
u\cdot a_{1}/c_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we deduce that
\mathfrak{G}_{(2)}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c},u;0,-\frac{2aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}\right)
is integral-parametric if and only if
\gcd\left(u,\frac{u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}\right)\mid\frac{uN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{c}
. Suppose this is the case. If
c_{1}
is odd or
4\mid c_{1}
, then (
6.2
) can be calculated using (
1.4
) or (
1.5
), respectively, with
G=(2)
. Otherwise, if
c_{1}\equiv 2\bmod{4}
and
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\mid aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}
, then (
6.2
) equals
0
by, e.g.,
[
6
, Theorem 1.5.1]
. If
c_{1}\equiv 2\bmod{4}
and
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\nmid aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}
, then (
6.2
) can be calculated efficiently using
[
6
, Theorem 1.2.2]
. However, if
\gcd\left(u,\frac{u\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}\right)\nmid\frac{uN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{c}
, i.e., (
6.2
) is not integral-parametric, we do not know any explicit formula for it.

Proof.

Set
H=\{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\colon\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{h}\}
, which is a subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. Then
H=\langle\mathbf{h}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rangle^{\bot}
, and hence by Lemma
2.3
,
H^{\bot}=\langle\mathbf{h}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rangle
, the subgroup generated by
\mathbf{h}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. It is immediate that
\lvert H^{\bot}\rvert=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathbf{h}+c\mathbb{Z}^{n})=u
, and consequently
\lvert H\rvert=c^{n}/u
by Lemma
2.3
.

By Theorem
1.3
, we have

(6.3)

\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c\mathbb{Z}^{n},\,\mathbf{v}\perp\mathbf{h}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{c}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})^{\mathrm{T}}\cdot G\cdot(\mathbf{v}+\mathbf{w})\right)\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{G}^{H}(a/c;\mathbf{w})\\
=c^{n/2}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)u^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w}).

It remains to calculate
\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w})
. Expanding the definition, we find that

(6.4)

\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w})=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{2}a^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}}{2c}\right)\sum_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}v^{2}-\frac{aa^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{c}v\right).

If
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\equiv 0\bmod{2c}
, then

\mathfrak{G}_{G^{\bot}}^{H^{\bot}}(a^{\bot}/c;-aG\mathbf{w})=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{2}a^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}}{2c}\right)\sum_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{aa^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{c}v\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{2}a^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}}{2c}\right)uC.

(When
\mathbf{h}\not\perp N\mathbf{w}
, the last equality follows from the fact
c\mid uN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}
.) Inserting this into (
6.3
), and noting

(6.5)

\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a}{2c}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{2}a^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}}{2c}\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}\right),

we arrive at (
6.1
) in the case
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\equiv 0\bmod{2c}
. On the other hand, if
\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}\not\equiv 0\bmod{2c}
, (
6.1
) follows from (
6.3
)–(
6.5
), and

\sum_{v=0}^{u-1}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c}v^{2}-\frac{aa^{\bot}N\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{c}v\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{(2)}\left(\frac{a^{\bot}\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}{2c},u;0,-\frac{2aN\mathbf{w}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbf{h}}{\mathbf{h}^{\mathrm{T}}G^{\bot}\mathbf{h}}\right).

This concludes the proof.
∎

6.4.
Hecke Gauss sums

Throughout this subsection, let
K
be a number field,
\mathcal{O}
be the integral closure of
\mathbb{Z}
in
K
, and let
\mathfrak{d}
and
\Delta
be the different and discriminant of
K
, respectively. Let
(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{n})
be an integral basis of
K
, i.e.,
\mathcal{O}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{Z}b_{j}
and
K=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{Q}b_{j}
. Let
\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\colon K\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
and
\mathbf{N}\colon K\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
be the (absolute) trace and norm of
K
, respectively. For a fractional ideal
\mathfrak{a}
, let
\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{\mathfrak{a}})
be its norm. Note that
\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{\mathfrak{a}})=\lvert\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{a}\rvert
if
\mathfrak{a}
is integral, that
\mathbf{N}(\omega)=\mathbf{N}(\mathcal{O}\omega)
if
\omega\in K^{\times}=K\setminus\{0\}
, and that
\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{d})=\lvert\Delta\rvert
; cf., e.g.
[
24
, Prop. 22 in p. 26 and Prop. 14 in p. 66]
. Set
n=[K\colon\mathbb{Q}]
.

Let
\omega\in K^{\times}
. Then there are unique coprime integral ideals
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{b}
such that
\omega\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
. The
Hecke Gauss sum
is defined by the formula

C(\omega)=C_{K}(\omega):=\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu^{2}\omega)\right).

See
[
10
, Section 1]
for a good historical overview. As was mentioned by Boylan and Skoruppa in this paper, no explicit formulas were known for
C_{K}(\omega)
if
K\neq\mathbb{Q}
until they derived one for
K
being quadratic fields. See also
[
11
]
, where the authors provided an elegant proof of Hecke’s reciprocity law for
C(\omega)
, based on Milgram’s formula.

We shall show that for all
K
and all
\omega
,
C(\omega)
is equal to some
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(r,\mathbf{t};\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})
, up to a simple factor, where
G
is a nonsingular even integral symmetric matrix of size
n
depending on
K
and
\omega
. Then, in the case of quadratic fields, we derive an explicit formula (different from Boylan and Skoruppa’s) when Theorem
4.6
can be applied. Finally, we provide an example where
K
is a cyclotomic field.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
c_{1}
be a positive integer such that
c_{1}\mathcal{O}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}
. Let
a_{1}
be the greatest common divisor of
\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(c_{1}\omega b_{j}^{2})
(
j=1,2,\dots,n
) and
2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(c_{1}\omega b_{i}b_{j})
(
1\leq i<j\leq n
). Set
c=c_{1}/\gcd(a_{1},c_{1})
,
a=a_{1}/\gcd(a_{1},c_{1})
, and
G=\left(2a^{-1}c\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\omega b_{i}b_{j})\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
. Then
G
is a nonsingular even integral symmetric matrix (independent of the choice of
c_{1}
, but depending on the basis
(b_{j})
), and

(6.6)

C_{K}(\omega)=\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})c^{-n}\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{0}).

Proof.

(1)
c_{1}
exists since
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathfrak{a}
are both lattices in
K
(
\cong_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}^{n}
).

(2) We now show
\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(c_{1}\omega b_{i}b_{j})\in\mathbb{Z}
, from which it follows that
a_{1}
is well-defined. The map
(x,y)\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(xy)
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear map on
K\times K
. It is strongly nondegenerate (cf., e.g.,
[
34
, Theorem 8.2.2]
). Moreover,
\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
, by the definition, is the dual lattice of
\mathcal{O}
in
(K,(x,y)\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(xy))
. Thus,

\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(c_{1}\omega b_{i}b_{j})\in\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\omega c_{1}\mathcal{O})\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\omega\mathfrak{a})=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{d}^{-1})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}.

(3) It is immediate that
G
is even integral and symmetric. Since
K=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{Q}b_{j}=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{Q}\omega b_{j}
, the nondegeneracy of
G
follows from the fact
(x,y)\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(xy)
is strongly nondegenerate. Moreover, a different choice of
c_{1}
leads to the same
a
and
c
, and hence the same
G
.

(4) We now show (
6.6
). We consider the sum
\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu^{2}\omega)\right)
, which is well-defined since for
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{O}
, the condition
\mu_{1}+c_{1}\mathcal{O}=\mu_{2}+c_{1}\mathcal{O}
would imply that

\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{1}^{2}\omega)\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{2}^{2}\omega)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(2\mu_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\omega)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}((\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})^{2}\omega)\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{2}^{2}\omega)\right),

as
2\mu_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\omega,\,(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})^{2}\omega\in\omega c_{1}\mathcal{O}\subseteq\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu^{2}\omega)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{a},\,\mu_{2}\in\mathfrak{a}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}((\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})^{2}\omega)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{1}^{2}\omega)\right)\sum_{\mu_{2}\in\mathfrak{a}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(2\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\omega)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{2}^{2}\omega)\right)

\displaystyle=\lvert\mathfrak{a}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}\rvert\cdot C_{K}(\omega),

since
\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(2\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\omega)+\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu_{2}^{2}\omega)\right)=1
as
\mu_{1}\in\mathcal{O}
and
\mu_{2}\in\mathfrak{a}
. It follows that

\displaystyle C_{K}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\lvert\mathfrak{a}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}\rvert^{-1}\sum_{\mu\in\mathcal{O}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mu^{2}\omega)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{a}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{O}/c_{1}\mathcal{O}\rvert}\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}/c_{1}\mathbb{Z}^{n}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}((v_{1}b_{1}+v_{2}b_{2}+\dots v_{n}b_{n})^{2}\omega)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})c_{1}^{-n}\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c,(c_{1},c_{1},\dots,c_{1});\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}).

Inserting (
3.2
) with
\mathbf{t}=(c_{1},c_{1},\dots,c_{1})
into the above formula, we obtain (
6.6
) as desired.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

We can choose
c_{1}
with merely
c_{1}\omega\in\mathcal{O}
, which is easier to handle. Then it follows that
c_{1}\omega\mathfrak{d}=c_{1}\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
is integral. Consequently,
\mathfrak{a}\mid c_{1}\mathfrak{b}
, and hence
\mathfrak{a}\mid c_{1}\mathcal{O}
, i.e.,
c_{1}\mathcal{O}\subseteq\mathfrak{a}
, since
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{b}
are coprime by the assumption. Therefore, Proposition
6.3
is valid for this
c_{1}
.

Remark 6.5
.

To compute the right-hand side of (
6.6
), we need to first work out
\mathfrak{a}
, since
\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})
appears. For certain
K
and
\omega
, we can know the value of
\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})
without knowing
\mathfrak{a}
. Assume that each prime factor
p
of
\Delta
does not split in
\mathcal{O}
, i.e., there is only one prime
\mathcal{O}
-ideal lying above
p\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\omega=\alpha/c_{1}
with
c_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
\alpha\in\mathcal{O}
. We further assume that
\gcd(c_{1},\mathbf{N}(\alpha))=1
. Then we would have

(6.7)

\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})=\frac{c_{1}^{n}}{\gcd(c_{1}^{n},\Delta)}.

To see this, suppose to the contrary that there is a rational prime
p
dividing
\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})
and
\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{b})
. Then there are prime
\mathcal{O}
-ideals
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}
lying above
p
such that
\mathfrak{p}_{1}\mid\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\mid\mathfrak{b}
. Since
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{b}
are coprime, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{1}\neq\mathfrak{p}_{2}
. Since
\omega\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
, i.e.,
\alpha\mathfrak{d}(c_{1}\mathcal{O})^{-1}=\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{1}\mid c_{1}\mathcal{O}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\mid\alpha\mathfrak{d}
. The assumption
\gcd(c_{1},\mathbf{N}(\alpha))=1
implies
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\nmid\alpha\mathcal{O}
, so
\mathfrak{p}_{2}\mid\mathfrak{d}
. Taking the norms we find
p\mid\Delta
, which contradicts the assumption on
\Delta
. We have thus proved
\gcd(\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a}),\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{b}))=1
. Taking the norms of
\alpha\mathfrak{d}(c_{1}\mathcal{O})^{-1}=\mathfrak{b}\mathfrak{a}^{-1}
, we have
\mathbf{N}(\alpha)\lvert\Delta\rvert c_{1}^{-n}=\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{b})\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})^{-1}
, from which (
6.7
) follows.

Remark 6.6
.

Set
T_{K}(\omega)=\left(\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\omega b_{i}b_{j})\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
, then
G=2a^{-1}cT_{K}(\omega)
. To compute the right-hand side of (
6.6
) in practice, we need to work out
T_{K}(\omega)
. Let
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\dots,\theta_{n}
be all field embeddings from
K
to
\mathbb{C}
that fix
\mathbb{Q}
. Then
\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\theta_{j}(x)
for
x\in K
. Set
B=(\theta_{j}(b_{i}))_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
and
W=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\theta_{1}(\omega),\dots,\theta_{n}(\omega))
. It is immediate that

T_{K}(\omega)=BWB^{\mathrm{T}}.

For quadratic fields, Proposition
6.3
specializes to the following one.

Proposition 6.7
.

Let
d\neq 0,1
be a square-free integer,
K=\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})
, and let
\omega=\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}{c_{1}}
where
c_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
v_{0},v_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\gcd(c_{1},v_{0},v_{1})=1
. Set

(6.8)

a_{1}=\begin{cases}\gcd(2v_{0},4v_{1}d)&\text{if }d\not\equiv 1\bmod{4}\\
\gcd(2v_{0},2v_{1}d,v_{0}+v_{1}d)&\text{if }d\equiv 1\bmod{4},\end{cases}

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}{4v_{0}a_{1}^{-1}}&{4v_{1}da_{1}^{-1}}\\
{4v_{1}da_{1}^{-1}}&{4v_{0}da_{1}^{-1}}\end{matrix}\right)\text{ if }d\not\equiv 1\bmod{4},

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{matrix}{4v_{0}a_{1}^{-1}}&{2(v_{0}+v_{1}d)a_{1}^{-1}}\\
{2(v_{0}+v_{1}d)a_{1}^{-1}}&{(v_{0}d+v_{0}+2v_{1}d)a_{1}^{-1}}\end{matrix}\right)\text{ if }d\equiv 1\bmod{4}.

Set
a=a_{1}/\gcd(a_{1},c_{1}),\,c=c_{1}/\gcd(a_{1},c_{1})
. Then
G
is nonsingular, even integral, and symmetric, and we have

(6.9)

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}\left(\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}{c_{1}}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})}{c^{2}}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\left(\frac{a}{c};\mathbf{0}\right).

In particular, if
\gcd(c_{1},v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)=1
, then

(6.10)

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}\left(\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}{c_{1}}\right)=\frac{(c_{1},a_{1})^{2}}{(c_{1}^{2},\Delta)}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\left(\frac{a}{c};\mathbf{0}\right),

where
\Delta
is the discriminant of
\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})
(
\Delta=d
if
d\equiv 1\bmod{4}
, and
\Delta=4d
otherwise).

Proof.

We take the integral basis
b_{1}=1
,
b_{2}=\delta
, where
\delta=(1+\sqrt{d})/2
if
d\equiv 1\bmod{4}
and
\delta=\sqrt{d}
otherwise. Let
T_{K}(\omega)
,
B
and
W
be as in Remark
6.6
. For
K=\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})
,
\theta_{1}
is the identity map and
\theta_{2}
maps
r_{1}+r_{2}\sqrt{d}
to
\overline{r_{1}+r_{2}\sqrt{d}}=r_{1}-r_{2}\sqrt{d}
, where
r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}
. Then
B=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{1}&{1}\\
{\delta}&{\overline{\delta}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
,
W=c_{1}^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}&{0}\\
{0}&{v_{0}-v_{1}\sqrt{d}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and hence
T_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}(\omega)=c_{1}^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{2v_{0}}&{2v_{1}d}\\
{2v_{1}d}&{2v_{0}d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
if
d\not\equiv 1\bmod{4}
, and
T_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}(\omega)=c_{1}^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{2v_{0}}&{v_{0}+v_{1}d}\\
{v_{0}+v_{1}d}&{v_{0}d/2+v_{0}/2+v_{1}d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
if
d\equiv 1\bmod{4}
. The quantity
a_{1}
defined by (
6.8
) is the same as the one defined in Proposition
6.3
, and thus
G
defined here is the same as in Proposition
6.3
. Therefore, the conclusion on
G
and (
6.9
) follow from Proposition
6.3
. Now suppose that
\gcd(c_{1},v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)=1
, that is,
\gcd(c_{1},\mathbf{N}(v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}))=1
. Since each prime factor
p
of
\Delta
does not split in
\mathcal{O}
for
K=\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})
, we conclude by Remark
6.5
and (
6.9
) that (
6.10
) holds.
∎

Theorem 6.8
.

Let the notations and assumptions be as in Proposition
6.7
. Suppose that

\gcd(c,4\Delta(v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)/a_{1}^{2})=1.

Then we have

(6.11)

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}\left(\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}{c_{1}}\right)=\frac{\mathbf{N}(\mathfrak{a})}{c}\cdot(-1)^{\frac{1-c_{0}}{2}}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{4\Delta(v_0^2-v_1^2d)/a_1^2}{c}_{K},

where
c_{0}
is the odd part of
c
. If in addition
\gcd(c_{1},v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)=1
, then

(6.12)

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{d})}\left(\frac{v_{0}+v_{1}\sqrt{d}}{c_{1}}\right)=\frac{c_{1}(c_{1},a_{1})}{(c_{1}^{2},\Delta)}\cdot(-1)^{\frac{1-c_{0}}{2}}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{4\Delta(v_0^2-v_1^2d)/a_1^2}{c}_{K}.

Proof.

We have
\det(G)=4\Delta(v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)/a_{1}^{2}
. Thus the assumption
\gcd(c,4\Delta(v_{0}^{2}-v_{1}^{2}d)/a_{1}^{2})=1
means
\gcd(c,\det(G))=1
. Since the determinant and level of
G
have the same set of prime factors, Theorem
4.6
can be applied to
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{0})
. Therefore, we insert (
1.4
) into the right-hand sides of (
6.9
) and (
6.10
), and obtain (
6.11
) and (
6.12
), respectively.
∎

To conclude this subsection, we present a specialization of Proposition
6.3
and an example showing that one can compute the exact value of
C_{K}(\omega)
, where
K
is a cyclotomic field, by combining Theorem
4.6
and this specialization.

Let
p
be an odd rational prime,
\zeta_{p}=\mathfrak{e}\left(1/p\right)
, and
K=\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})
. Then
n=[K\colon\mathbb{Q}]=p-1
,
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{p}]
,
p\mathcal{O}=\mathfrak{p}^{p-1}
,
\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{p}^{p-2}
, and
\Delta=\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{-1}{p}_{K}\cdot p^{p-2}
, where
\mathfrak{p}
is the unique prime
\mathcal{O}
-ideal lying above
p\mathbb{Z}
(indeed,
\mathfrak{p}
is the principal ideal generated by
1-\zeta_{p}
); cf.
[
24
, Chap. 4]
. We shall use the following integral basis

(b_{1},b_{2},\dots,b_{p-1})=(\zeta_{p}^{1},\zeta_{p}^{2},\dots,\zeta_{p}^{p-1}).

For each
1\leq k\leq p-1
, define a
(p-1)\times(p-1)
matrix
T_{k}
by the formula

\text{the }(i_{1},i_{2})\text{ entry}=T_{k}[i_{1},i_{2}]=\begin{cases}p-1&\text{if }i_{1}+i_{2}\equiv-k\bmod{p}\\
-1&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proposition 6.9
.

In Proposition
6.3
, if
K=\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})
and
\omega=c_{1}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}v_{k}\zeta_{p}^{k}
, where
c_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and
v_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
\gcd(c_{1},v_{1},\dots,v_{p-1})=1
, then
a_{1}
is equal to the greatest common divisor of

\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}v_{k}T_{k}[j,j],\text{ where }j=1,2,\dots,p-1,\text{ and }\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}2v_{k}T_{k}[i,j],\text{ where }1\leq i<j\leq p-1,

and we have

G=2a_{1}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}v_{k}T_{k}.

Moreover, if we assume additionally
\gcd(c_{1},\mathbf{N}(\sum v_{k}\zeta_{p}^{k}))=1
, then (
6.6
) becomes

(6.13)

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}\left(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}v_{k}\zeta_{p}^{k}}{c_{1}}\right)=\frac{(c_{1},a_{1})^{p-1}}{(c_{1}^{p-1},p^{p-2})}\mathfrak{G}_{G}\left(\frac{a}{c};\mathbf{0}\right).

Proof.

Let
B
,
W
, and
T_{K}(\omega)
be the matrices defined in Remark
6.6
. The Galois group of
K=\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})
over
\mathbb{Q}
consists of
p-1
elements:
\theta_{j}
,
j=1,2,\dots,p-1
, where
\theta_{j}(\zeta_{p})=\zeta_{p}^{j}
; cf.
[
24
, p. 71]
. It follows that
B=\left(\mathfrak{e}\left(ij/p\right)\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq p-1}
. A direct calculation shows that

T_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{p})}(\omega)=\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}c_{1}^{-1}v_{k}B\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\zeta_{p}^{k},\zeta_{p}^{2k},\dots\zeta_{p}^{(p-1)k})B^{\mathrm{T}}=c_{1}^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}v_{k}T_{k}.

The assertions on
a_{1}
and
G
thus follow from
G=2a^{-1}cT_{K}(\omega)
. The last assertion follows from Remark
6.5
and the fact
p
does not split in
\mathbb{Z}[\zeta_{p}]
.
∎

Example 6.10
.

For
p=13
and
\omega=37^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{12}k\zeta_{13}^{k}\in\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{13})
, we have
a_{1}=13
,
c_{1}=37
,
a=13
,
c=37
, and

G=\begin{pmatrix}10&8&6&4&2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12\\
8&6&4&2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12\\
6&4&2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10\\
4&2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8\\
2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6\\
0&-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4\\
-2&-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4&2\\
-4&-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4&2&0\\
-6&-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4&2&0&-2\\
-8&-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4&2&0&-2&-4\\
-10&-12&12&10&8&6&4&2&0&-2&-4&-6\\
-12&12&10&8&6&4&2&0&-2&-4&-6&-8\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\det(G)=2^{12}13^{10}
and
\mathbf{N}(\sum_{k=1}^{12}k\zeta_{13}^{k})=13^{11}
, we have
\gcd(c,\det(G))=1
and
\gcd(c_{1},\mathbf{N}(\sum k\zeta_{13}^{k}))=1
, respectively, and hence we can substitute (
1.4
) into the right-hand side of (
6.13
), which is ensured by Theorem
4.6
. We thus obtain

C_{\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_{13})}\left(\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{12}k\zeta_{13}^{k}}{37}\right)=\mathfrak{G}_{G}\left(\frac{13}{37};\mathbf{0}\right)=37^{6}.

7.
Application II. Explicit formulas for coefficients of Weil representations of
\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})

In this section, we first prove Theorem
1.4
, then present an explicit formula in the case
N\mid c
, and finally translate these formulas into the language of theta series of lattice index. For the definitions and basic properties, see §
2.1
.

7.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

Choose any
\mathbb{Z}
-linear isomorphism
\sigma\colon\mathbb{Z}^{n}\rightarrow L
, and set
G=\left(B(\sigma(e_{i}),\sigma(e_{j})\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
, where
e_{i}
is the
i
th column of the identity matrix. Then
G
is a positive definite even integral symmetric matrix, and
\sigma
is an isometry from
(\mathbb{Z}^{n},(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\mapsto\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w})
onto
(L,B)
. Thus, without loss of generality we assume
L=\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and
B(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})=\mathbf{v}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{w}
. Note that
\det(G)=\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert
. Let
\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}\in L^{\sharp}
be any representatives of
w,v\in L^{\sharp}/L
, respectively. Setting in (
3.3
)
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{1}
and
\mathbf{x}=-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1}
, we derive that

(7.1)

\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}=\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1}}=D^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}}c^{-\frac{n}{2}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{d}{2c}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w}_{1},-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1}).

Note that
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w}_{1},-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1})
is integral-parametric, and that
\gcd(N,c)=1
. (
N
is defined as the level of
\underline{L}
, so is also that of
G
.) This means Theorem
1.1
(1) is valid for
\mathfrak{G}_{G}(a/c;\mathbf{w}_{1},-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1})
. Inserting (
1.4
) (with
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{1}
and
\mathbf{x}=-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1}
) into the right-hand side of (
7.1
), we obtain

(7.2)

\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{D}}\cdot(-\mathrm{i})^{\frac{n}{2}}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right)\\
\times\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{d}{2c}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{1}{2ac}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{v}_{1}+\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}_{1}-a^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}_{1}-a^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{1})\right).

A direct calculation shows that (using
ad-bc=1
)

\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{d}{2c}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{1}{2ac}\mathbf{v}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}G\mathbf{v}_{1}+\frac{ac^{\prime}}{2}(\mathbf{w}_{1}-a^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{1})^{\mathrm{T}}G(\mathbf{w}_{1}-a^{-1}\mathbf{v}_{1})\right)\\
=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a}Q(\mathbf{v}_{1})-c^{\prime}B(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})+ac^{\prime}Q(\mathbf{w}_{1})\right),

from which and (
7.2
) the desired formula follows.

Remark 7.1
.

The root of unity
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a}Q(\mathbf{v}_{1})-c^{\prime}B(\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{1})+ac^{\prime}Q(\mathbf{w}_{1})\right)
is independent of the choices of representatives
\mathbf{w}_{1}
and
\mathbf{v}_{1}
, so we are safe to write just
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a}Q(v)-c^{\prime}B(w,v)+ac^{\prime}Q(w)\right)
. This can be seen from the above proof, or alternatively, from the expression itself, since we have
\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a}\in\mathbb{Z}
, which follows from
a\mid bc+cc^{\prime}
.

7.2.
The formula in the case
N\mid c

Using (
1.5
) instead of (
1.4
) we have the following analog of Theorem
1.4
in the case
N\mid c
. This is not a new result; see Remark
7.3
.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
\underline{L}=(L,B)
be a positive definite even integral lattice of rank
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
and set
Q(x)=\frac{1}{2}B(x,x)
. Let
A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
with
N\mid c>0
, where
N
is the level of
\underline{L}
. Then for
w,v\in L^{\sharp}/L
we have

(7.3)

\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}=\delta_{aw,v}\cdot\mu_{G}(a,c)\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(abQ(w)\right),

where
\mu_{G}(a,c)
is defined by (
1.6
) with
D=\lvert L^{\sharp}/L\rvert
, and where
\delta_{aw,v}
is the Kronecker
\delta
.

Proof.

If
a=0
, then
c=1
and hence
N=1
,
L^{\sharp}=L
. Then
8\mid n
by Theorem
3.19
, and consequently (
7.3
) holds by (
2.2
). Now assume
a\neq 0
. As in the last subsection, (
7.1
) still holds in the present case. Inserting (
1.5
) (with
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{w}_{1}
and
\mathbf{x}=-a^{-1}G\mathbf{v}_{1}
) into the right-hand side of (
7.1
), we obtain

\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}=\delta_{aw,v}\cdot\mu_{G}(a,c)\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{d-a^{\prime}}{c}Q(v)\right),

where
a^{\prime}
satisfies
aa^{\prime}\equiv 1\bmod{cN}
. The desired formula (
7.3
) follows from this and the fact when
v=aw
we have
\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{d-a^{\prime}}{c}Q(v)\right)=\mathfrak{e}\left(abQ(w)\right)
.
∎

Remark 7.3
.

An explicit formula for
\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}
in the case
N\mid c
, to our best knowledge, was first obtained, in the form of a transformation equation of certain theta series (see the next subsection), by Schoeneberg
[
38
, Eq. (16)]
, using a method developed by Hecke. Schoeneberg considered the case
2\mid n
, and the case
2\nmid n
was treated by Pfezter
[
31
, Eq. (22)]
. For more recent reference, see
[
17
, Theorem 14.3.11]
. Note that our formula are different from the above three formulas mentioned (e.g.,
\mu_{G}(a,c)
depends on
a
and
c
, while the formula in
[
17
, Theorem 14.3.11]
expresses
\mu_{G}(a,c)
in another form depending on
c
and
d
), and the proof of the equivalence of these formulas is not very obvious. Finally, note that Richter’s results
[
32
,
33
]
on symplectic theta series associated with possibly indefinite lattices can also be applied to obtain a formula for
\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(A,1\right)_{w,v}
.

7.3.
Jacobi theta series of lattice index

Let
\underline{L}=(L,B)
, where
L\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, be a positive definite even integral lattice. Set
Q(x)=\frac{1}{2}B(x,x)
and extend
B
to
\mathbb{C}^{n}
by bilinearity. We consider the following theta series

(7.4)

\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}(\tau,z)=\sum_{v\in t+L}\mathfrak{e}\left(\tau Q(v)+B(v,z)\right),

where
\tau\in\mathfrak{H}:=\{\tau\in\mathbb{C}\colon\Im\tau>0\}
,
z\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, and
t\in L^{\sharp}
. It is well-known that this series converges absolutely and uniformly on compact subsets, and hence is holomorphic. Moreover,
\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}
depends only on
t+L
, not
t
itself, so we may assume
t\in L^{\sharp}/L
. A good reference is
[
12
, §3.5]
, where Boylan developed the transformation equations of
\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}
for
\mathcal{O}
-lattice
\underline{L}
, based on
[
18
, Chap. 5]
. By setting
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{Z}
, the functions considered by Boylan become (
7.4
). See
[
2
, §2.3]
as well for another good reference. The modular transformation equations of
\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}
are well-known:

(7.5)

\varepsilon^{-n}(c\tau+d)^{-n/2}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{c}{c\tau+d}Q(z)\right)\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}\left(\frac{a\tau+b}{c\tau+d},\frac{z}{c\tau+d}\right)=\sum_{x\in L^{\sharp}/L}\rho_{\underline{L}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right),\varepsilon\right)_{t,x}\vartheta_{\underline{L},x}(\tau,z),

where
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
and
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
. See for instance
[
2
, Theorem 2.3.4]
. Therefore, Theorem
1.4
has an equivalent form, which provides explicitly the Fourier expansion of
\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}\left(\frac{a\tau+b}{c\tau+d},\frac{z}{c\tau+d}\right)
.

Theorem 7.4
.

If
c>0
is coprime to
N
, the level of
\underline{L}
, and
a\neq 0
, then

(7.6)

(c\tau+d)^{-n/2}\mathfrak{e}\left(-\frac{c}{c\tau+d}Q(z)\right)\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}\left(\frac{a\tau+b}{c\tau+d},\frac{z}{c\tau+d}\right)=\gamma\cdot\sum_{v\in L^{\sharp}}c(v,t)q^{Q(v)}\zeta^{v},

where
q^{Q(v)}:=\mathfrak{e}\left(\tau Q(v)\right)
,
\zeta^{v}:=\mathfrak{e}\left(B(v,z)\right)
, and where

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{D}}\cdot(-\mathrm{i})^{\frac{n}{2}}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{\abs{a}}{c}_{K}^{n}\cdot\genfrac{(}{)}{}{}{D}{c}_{K}\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{n\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sgn}}\left(a\right)\cdot(1-c_{0})}{8}\right),

\displaystyle c(v,t)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}\left(\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a}Q(v)-c^{\prime}B(t,v)+ac^{\prime}Q(t)\right).

(For
D
,
c^{\prime}
and
c_{0}
, see Theorem
1.4
.)

Proof.

This follows from combining Theorem
1.4
and (
7.5
).
∎

Remark 7.5
.

If we set
\vartheta_{\underline{L}^{\sharp}}(\tau,z)=\sum_{v\in L^{\sharp}}\mathfrak{e}\left(\tau Q(v)+B(v,z)\right)
, or equivalently,
\vartheta_{\underline{L}^{\sharp}}=\sum_{t\in L^{\sharp}/L}\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}
, then the right-hand side of (
7.6
) can be rewritten as

\gamma\cdot\mathfrak{e}\left(ac^{\prime}Q(t)\right)\cdot\vartheta_{\underline{L}^{\sharp}}\left(\tau+\frac{b+c^{\prime}}{a},z-c^{\prime}t\right).

The formula (
7.6
), with the right-hand side replaced by the above expression, shows a phenomenon that the action of a modular transformation
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}{a}&{b}\\
{c}&{d}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
on
\vartheta_{\underline{L},t}
is equivalent to the action of the translation
(\tau,z)\mapsto(\tau+(b+c^{\prime})/a,z-c^{\prime}t)
on
\vartheta_{\underline{L}^{\sharp}}
, up to a factor in
\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{D},\zeta_{N},\zeta_{8})
. It is an interesting question, at least to us, to study whether, for any modular (or Jacobi) form, there is a function that plays the same role as
\vartheta_{\underline{L}^{\sharp}}
plays for
